Towards security automation in Software Defined Networks
page=1 block=22873252 (x=1 y=132) width=349.000000 height=9.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Noe M. Yungaicela-Naula a,?, Cesar Vargas-Rosalesa, Jesús Arturo Pérez-Díaz a, Mahdi Zareei a
page=1 block=28378447 (x=1 y=151) width=433.000000 height=9.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
aTecnologico de Monterrey, School of Engineering and Sciences, Monterrey, NL 64849 MX, Mexico
page=1 block=18023402 (x=1 y=167) width=275.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
A R T I C L E I N F O
page=1 block=6161749 (x=1 y=195) width=94.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=2 wordint.max=12
A B S T R A C T
page=1 block=713688405 (x=165 y=195) width=66.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=2 wordint.max=6
Keywords:
page=1 block=1770332 (x=1 y=215) width=27.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
Software-Defined Networking (SDN) is a modern paradigm that provides a platform for implementing reliable,
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=215) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
centrally managed, and automated security solutions for conventional and new generation networks, such as
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=224) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
IoT, cloud computing, 5G/6G mobile communication networks, and vehicular communications. In these com-
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=234) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
plex systems, manual security operations can delay or obstruct the identification, mitigation, and prevention
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=243) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of ever-increasing sophisticated threats. Thus, the idea of security automation for networks using the SDN
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=253) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
paradigm has become fundamental, given that SDN was created to facilitate the operation and management
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=262) width=354.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of complex networks with minimal human intervention, which is considered error-prone. This survey studies
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=272) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the state-of-the-art research efforts concerned with security automation in SDN environments. We identified
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=282) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and ranked various classes of security solutions with different levels of automation and complexity. The level
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=291) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of automation is measured using four well-defined qualitative parameters: self-healing, self-adaptation, self-
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=301) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
configuration, and self-optimization. The complexity is characterized by the amount of processing and storage
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=310) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
resources and implementation requirements. This work represents the first endeavor to analyze the level of
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=320) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
automation and complexity of security solutions in SDN environments. Our findings reveal important advances
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=329) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in the area of security automation in SDN. However, there are still several open problems and challenges, which
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=339) width=354.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
we detail in this work.
page=1 block=-466979651 (x=165 y=349) width=74.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Software defined networks
page=1 block=5843408 (x=1 y=223) width=75.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=1 block=5843408 (x=1 y=241) width=89.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
Machinelearning
page=1 block=5843408 (x=1 y=249) width=49.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
Deeplearning
page=1 block=5843408 (x=1 y=258) width=39.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
Reinforcementlearning

1. Introduction
page=2 block=3867800 (x=1 y=196) width=59.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Security demands in new complex and dynamic networks, such as
page=2 block=16555866 (x=13 y=217) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
IoT [1–4], cloud computing [5], 5G/6G communication networks[6,7],
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=227) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and vehicular communications, [8,9], in addition to the increasing
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=237) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
number of sophisticated and intelligent attacks, are key motivating
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=248) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
factors for deploying automated defense solutions for networks using
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=258) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the Software-Defined Networking (SDN) technology. On the one hand,
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=268) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
key networking trends place an increasing burden on systems and
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=279) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
network administrators. These trends include an increase in network
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=289) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
traffic volume, the virtualization of servers, storage, and networking
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=299) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
devices, the growth in size and complexity of data centers, and the
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=310) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
growth of cloud computing and IoT applications. In this context, the
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=320) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Information Technology (IT) workforce becomes a significant security
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=330) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
bottleneck. This workforce cannot manage the vastly increasing num-
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=340) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ber of incidents and alerts and the need to deploy optimized security
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=351) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
controls in response. Manual procedures result in slower detection
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=361) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and remediation of issues, mistakes in resource configuration, and
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=371) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
erratic security policy implementation, leaving systems vulnerable to
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=382) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
compliance issues and threats.
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=392) width=108.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
On top of that, advanced security skills are in higher demand
page=2 block=16555866 (x=13 y=402) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
than ever before; however, there is a significant lack of qualified and
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=413) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
skilled security professionals. An estimated 3.5 million cybersecurity
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=423) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
jobs will be available but unfilled by 2021 [10]. In addition, existing
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=433) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cybersecurity staff is overworked and not set up for 24x7x365 incom-
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=444) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ing threats [11]. According to the CISCO Cybersecurity Report Series
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=454) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
2020 [12], from a survey of 2800 organizations around 13 countries,
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=464) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
40% of these were receiving 5000 daily alerts. This overwhelming
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=474) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
number of alerts is having an impact onsecurity administrators’ fatigue.
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=485) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
As a result, according to a report presented by the Enterprise Strategy
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=495) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Group [13], IT teams ignore about 74% of security threat alerts, even
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
when they have security solutions in place, due to the sheer volume.
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=516) width=245.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
On the other hand, agile and sophisticated threats are rising, such as
page=2 block=16555866 (x=13 y=526) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the Advanced Persistent Threats (APTs) [14]. Different from common
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cyber-attacks, APTs focus on large organizations and industry sectors,
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=547) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
causing severe damage, such as failure of essential services and destruc-
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=557) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tion of critical infrastructure. These attacks usually become undetected,
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=567) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and the damage caused can be critical [15]. Among the most frequent,
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=577) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sophisticated, and dangerous APTs are DDoS attacks. According to a
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=588) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Kaspersky report [16], the number of DDoS attacks in 2020 almost
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=598) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
doubled the number of DDoS attacks of 2019. APTs can cause huge
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=608) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
capital and reputational losses to the victims. Indeed, the World Eco-
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=619) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
nomic Forum detected that cyberattacks are perceived as the #2 global
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=629) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
risk of concern to business leaders in advanced economies [17].
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=639) width=228.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Including automation in the heart of network security management
page=2 block=16555866 (x=13 y=650) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
will help to deal with the increasing number of alerts and skilled IT
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=660) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
administrators’ shortages. Security automation is the machine-based
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=670) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
execution of security actions to programmatically detect, investigate,
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=681) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mitigate, and prevent threats timely and efficiently with minimal hu-
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
man intervention. With this automation, repetitive and time-consuming
page=2 block=16555866 (x=1 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
activities are taken out of the hands of the IT workforce so they can
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=187) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
focus on more relevant value-adding work.
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=197) width=153.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Security automation can reduce the average cost of a breach by
page=2 block=146504664 (x=282 y=207) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
95% [18]. Therefore, most organizations plan to increase automation
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=218) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to simplify and speed up response times in their security ecosystems.
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=228) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
According to Gartner, security automation is one of the top security
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=238) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and risk management trends [19]. The execution of automated security
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=249) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tasks is much faster than human interactions and generates fewer
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=259) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
errors.
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=269) width=23.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
SDN is enabling security managers to bridge the response resource
page=2 block=146504664 (x=282 y=279) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
gap through intelligent incident detection and automated response.
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=290) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
SDN decouples the control plane from the network devices and logically
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=300) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
centralizes the administration of network resources. This networking
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=310) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
technology presents new security challenges for network designers and
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=321) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
operators, but it also provides a platform for implementing reliable,
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=331) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
centrally managed, and automated security solutions for networks.
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=341) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Although many surveys have already studied intelligent meth-
page=2 block=146504664 (x=282 y=351) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ods applied in SDN-based security solutions, none have comprehen-
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=362) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sively analyzed and compared the complexity and level of automation
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=372) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
achieved with these techniques. The idea of security automation in
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=382) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
SDN environments is fundamental since this technology was created
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=393) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
to facilitate network operation and management with minimal human
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=403) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
intervention, which is considered error-prone.
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=413) width=164.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Network automation has been studied from different perspectives
page=2 block=146504664 (x=282 y=423) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and defined using terms such as cognitive, self-managing, or self-aware
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=434) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
networks [20–22]. The common characteristics of these definitions are
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=444) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the ability to think, learn, and remember. Therefore, an automated
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=454) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
network can adapt in response to conditions or events based on the
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=465) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
reasoning and prior knowledge it has obtained. Moreover, an auto-
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=475) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
mated network can be characterized by its ability to complete its
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=485) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tasks autonomously by using its self-properties, namely, self-healing,
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=495) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
self-adaptation, self-configuration, and self-optimization, to adapt dy-
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=506) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
namically to changing requirements while considering the end-to-end
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=516) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
goals [22].
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=526) width=38.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
In this paper, we survey existing research efforts related to the
page=2 block=146504664 (x=282 y=537) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
automation of security in SDN-based networks, using peer-reviewed
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=547) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
studies published in the last seven years (2015–2021).
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=557) width=194.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The security solutions reviewed are divided into two main classes,
page=2 block=146504664 (x=282 y=567) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
namely reactive and proactive solutions. On one hand, reactive security
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=578) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
solutions (also known as detection/mitigation solutions) are based on
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=588) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
different Artificial Intelligence (AI) enabled techniques such as Ma-
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=598) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
chine Learning (ML), Deep Learning (DL), and Reinforcement Learning
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=609) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(RL) with online or offline model learning. The methods of AI have
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=619) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
been used to discover threats in networks [23–25] since they are
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=629) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
considered as robust potential solutions to make networks self-aware,
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=639) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
self-adaptive, self-secured, self-healing, and self-managed [26,27]. The
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=650) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
study of SDN-based reactive security solutions requires an analysis of
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=660) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the characteristics that makes detection and mitigation mechanisms
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=670) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
adaptive to changes in the network. On the other hand, proactive
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=681) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
security solutions use emerging concepts such as Network Function Vir-
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tualization, Moving Target Defense, Cyber Deception, Network Slicing,
page=2 block=146504664 (x=270 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

defense methods focus on evade/prevent attacks or on reducing the

attack surfaces in the network. We evaluate the level of automa-

tion, of reactive and proactive solutions, in terms of their complexity

(self-healing, self-adaptation, self-configuration, and self-optimization).

The contributions of this work are:

opment.

parison of the complexity (processing, storage, and implemen-

class. Using these parameters, we present a ranking of the classes

prehensive and updated landscape of how the SDN paradigm

is empowering the creation of innovative and automated de-

possible areas of contribution in this imperative field of study.

operate in production environments.

area of security automation in SDN.
page=3 block=218383494 (x=24 y=271) width=129.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
This work represents the first attempt to study SDN-based security
page=3 block=16453871 (x=13 y=289) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
solutions from an automation perspective.
page=3 block=16453871 (x=1 y=299) width=150.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
1.1. Existing surveys
page=3 block=4589428 (x=1 y=318) width=70.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The problem of securing SDN networks has attracted a lot of atten-
page=3 block=16574258 (x=13 y=338) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tion. Many comprehensive reviews have been produced recently. These
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=348) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
reviews divided security and SDN into two categories: security for SDN,
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=358) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and SDN as a security solution for networks. In the first category,
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
security issues specific to the SDN architecture and their solutions have
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=378) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
been surveyed [28–31]. The most common security issues targeting the
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=388) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
SDN elements are the DoS/DDoS attacks. Therefore, many surveys focus
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=398) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
on these attacks and their solutions [32–35].
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=409) width=160.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In the second category, the SDN-based security solutions to protect
page=3 block=16574258 (x=13 y=419) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
traditional networks have been reviewed. The standard strategy to
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=429) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
review thesesolutions has been to divide them into statistics-based, ML-
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=439) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
based, DL-based, and metaheuristics-based solutions [23–25,36–38].
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=449) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
An alternative approach has been threat-oriented reviews [39–41].
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=459) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
These reviews define different types of existing threats to networks
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=469) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and study SDN-based solutions to these threats. Besides, several surveys
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=479) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
integrate the study of both categories, security for SDN and SDN-based
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=489) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
security solutions [26,42,43].
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=499) width=105.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Recent work in [20] reviews self-aware computer networks that in-
page=3 block=16574258 (x=13 y=509) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
clude those that propose the use of SDNs as a means to implement these
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=520) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
concepts. It analyzes the benefits of ML and RL mechanisms to implant
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=530) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
self-awareness into networks. Additionally, this study integrates the
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=540) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
QoS, energy, and security in a system. This system uses an RL-based
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=550) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
decision engine to achieve a self-aware network. However, this study
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=560) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
does not focus on SDN-based defense and does not analyze the level of
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=570) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
automation and complexity of the self-aware mechanisms.
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=580) width=207.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In contrast to previous surveys, we study SDN-based security solu-
page=3 block=16574258 (x=13 y=590) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tions for networks from an automationpoint of view. With thispurpose,
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=600) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
we propose a new taxonomy of security solutions according to different
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
levels of automation and complexity. The level of automation of these
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
techniques is evaluated and compared interms of their self-* properties.
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=631) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
No previous survey has studied the automation capabilities of SDN-
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=641) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
based security solutions. We also introduce a discussion about the
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=651) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
scalability and assessment factors of the SDN-based security systems,
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=661) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
which are fundamental in the design of complete, trustworthy, and
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=671) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ready to deploy automated solutions. Finally, we present the prob-
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=681) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
lems and challenges of current defense solutions and future research
page=3 block=16574258 (x=1 y=691) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
directions in the area of security automation in SDN environments.

1.2. Survey organization
page=3 block=1468663980 (x=270 y=370) width=83.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
This survey is organized as follows. Section 2 presents the back-
page=3 block=146450043 (x=282 y=391) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ground knowledge of the SDN architecture and the current state of
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=401) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the SDN technology. Section 3 introduces the taxonomy of the methods
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=411) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
searching for network defense automation using the SDN technology.
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=422) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Section 4 reviews the reactive security solutions for networks using
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=432) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
SDN and intelligent techniques. Section 5 reviews the state-of-the-
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
art proactive security solutions. Section 6 summarizes the findings
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=453) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
of the level of automation and complexity of reactive and proactive
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
solutions. Discussion and challenges are presented in Sections 7 and 8,
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
respectively. Finally, the conclusions are drawn in Section 9. Table 1

shows the list of the acronyms used in this survey.
page=3 block=146450043 (x=270 y=494) width=180.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
2. An overview of SDN
page=3 block=1539443215 (x=270 y=515) width=87.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SDN is a new networking paradigm where forwarding hardware
page=3 block=146499444 (x=282 y=535) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
is decoupled from control decisions. SDN provides new networking
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=546) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
capabilities: dynamic network programming, centralized network mon-
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=556) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
itoring, network virtualization, among others.
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=566) width=163.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Fig. 1 shows the general architecture of SDN. It has three planes: the
page=3 block=146499444 (x=282 y=577) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
data plane (DP), the control plane (CP), and the application plane (AP).
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=587) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The DP contains forwarding devices that forward, drop, and modify
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=597) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
packets based on policies received from the CP. The CP programs the
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=608) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
network resources, updates forwarding rules dynamically, and makes
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=618) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
network administration flexible and agile. The AP contains applications
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=629) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
which according to the business requirements, can implement the con-
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=639) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
trol logic to change the network behavior. Examples of applications are
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=649) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Network Virtualization, Security in Networks, Mobility Management,
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=660) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and Network Monitoring.
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=670) width=90.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Three interfaces are present in the SDN architecture. The south-
page=3 block=146499444 (x=282 y=680) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
bound interface (SBI), defined between the DP and the CP, permits
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
monitoring and notifies network events from the DP to the CP. Also,
page=3 block=146499444 (x=270 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 4 left:1 upper:1
N.M. Yungaicela-Naula, C. Vargas-Rosales, J.A. Pérez-Díaz et al.
page=4 block=11796484 (x=1 y=1) width=180.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Computer Communications 183 (2022) 64–82
page=4 block=-998244348 (x=393 y=1) width=128.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Example
page=4 block=1424491822 (x=152 y=39) width=25.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
SNMP, CLI, NETCONF
page=4 block=1424491822 (x=152 y=51) width=65.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
NVP (Nicira), Juniper’s Contrail, Cisco’s ACI
page=4 block=1424491822 (x=152 y=60) width=130.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Devices capable of running open-source software
page=4 block=1424491822 (x=152 y=68) width=143.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
67
page=4 block=117902879 (x=257 y=723) width=7.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
Table 2
page=4 block=40697936 (x=27 y=20) width=23.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SDN-like architectures.
page=4 block=164562156 (x=27 y=28) width=65.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Alternative
page=4 block=164562156 (x=28 y=39) width=32.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
SDN via APIS
page=4 block=164562156 (x=28 y=51) width=40.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
SDN via hypervisor
page=4 block=164562156 (x=28 y=60) width=57.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
SDN via opening up the device
page=4 block=164562156 (x=28 y=68) width=92.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SBI enables to forward control policies from the CP to the DP to provide
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=93) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
functions, such as programmatic control of devices. OpenFlow (OF) is
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=103) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the first and most popular protocol for SBI [44]. NetConf and SNMP
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=113) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
are other commonly used SBI APIs. The Northbound Interface (NBI),
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=124) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
defined between the CP and the AP, is used by the applications to ex-
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=134) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ploit the abstracted network provided by the CP to express the network
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=145) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
behavior. Rest, Java, and Python are examples of NBI APIs. Finally,
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=155) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the Eastbound/Westbound Interface (EWBI) allows communication in
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=166) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
multi-controller environments. The EWBI also enables the connection
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=176) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of the SDN controller to other network architectures.
page=4 block=16458743 (x=1 y=187) width=189.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
2.1. State of development of SDN technology
page=4 block=9962720 (x=1 y=208) width=152.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The original and ideal characteristics of the SDN paradigm are: con-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=13 y=229) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
trol plane separation, simplified forwarding device, centralized control,
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=240) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
network automation and virtualization, and openness [45]. The net-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=250) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
working paradigm with all these characteristics, hereafter, isreferred to
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=261) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
as Open SDN [45]. Open SDN was expected to bring significant benefits
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=271) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to networking, such as logically programmable infrastructure, central-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=282) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ized administration, dynamic and fast optimization of network opera-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=292) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tion and services, networking automation, and low-cost deployments.
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=303) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
OF-based SDN is an example of Open SDN.
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=313) width=152.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The OF-based SDN has several limitations [38,43,45–47]. The main
page=4 block=16558998 (x=13 y=324) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
limitations of this technology are that OF excessively decouples and
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=334) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
centralizes the control of the network and allows complete visibility.
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=345) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
These factors imply that all network traffic needs to be relayed to
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=355) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
a central controller. To implement novel solutions, such as traffic
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=366) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
management in high-performance networks, one has a difficult task
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=376) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
since it results unfeasible due to the bottleneck created in the controller
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=387) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and the switches. In addition, the controller is vulnerable to hard-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=397) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ware and software failures. Without the controller, the whole network
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=408) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lacks functionality. Solutions to these problems have been extensively
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=418) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
studied as established in different surveys [28–35].
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=428) width=182.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Alternatively, the SDN movement has led to the origin of SDN-like
page=4 block=16558998 (x=13 y=439) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
architectures to solve real and urgent networking problems and ren-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=449) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
der new and innovative network services in production environments.
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=460) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
These network overlay technologies were designed to create new com-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=470) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
petitive environments shifting the focus from physical infrastructure
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=481) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
to software features. They can provide great agility and automation,
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=491) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
simplified operational requirements, and extensive configuration func-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=502) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tion. These solutions can be seen as alternatives to Open SDN, which
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=512) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
address real-life problems. Table 2 shows these alternatives to SDN-like
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=523) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
solutions [45].
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=533) width=52.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
It is important to recognize that these alternative SDN-like solutions
page=4 block=16558998 (x=13 y=544) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
do not face all the issues and implementation challenges of the Open
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=554) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SDN. However, they present partial advantages of the original idea of
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=565) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the SDN paradigm. For instance, openness is not a characteristic of the
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=575) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
examples of SDN via hypervisor presented in Table 2. In this survey,
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=586) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
we include both Open SDN and SDN-like based security solutions.
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=596) width=235.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Current trends in SDN include leveraging new technologies such
page=4 block=16558998 (x=13 y=607) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
as P4 to increase network programmability and openness. P4, which
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=617) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stands for Programming Protocol-independent Packet Processors, pro-
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=628) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
vides high visibility and control of events in the data plane. This
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=638) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
technology releases network devices from closed protocols and runtime
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=649) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
APIs (such as OF). Moreover, P4 increases the action field of the
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=659) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
devices in the data plane, which reduces the SDN-controller processing
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=670) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
overhead. Thus, this technology boosts the overall SDN-based network
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
performance, enables deep network programmability, and amplifies the
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
spectrum of possible network applications [48].
page=4 block=16558998 (x=1 y=701) width=170.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Characteristics
page=4 block=-844692178 (x=327 y=39) width=42.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
Low changes, distributed, not open
page=4 block=-844692178 (x=327 y=51) width=103.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Network virtualization, automation, centralized control
page=4 block=-844692178 (x=327 y=60) width=161.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=4 block=-844692178 (x=327 y=68) width=74.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
3. Defense automation in SDN-based networks
page=4 block=-1145306584 (x=270 y=92) width=178.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Network security automation is a prominent area of study and
page=4 block=146428103 (x=282 y=113) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
is expected to be solved by modern network architectures, such as
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=124) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
SDN. We noticed different methods presented in the literature that
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=134) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
pursue the automation of defending networks using SDN and intelli-
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=144) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
gent techniques. To review such techniques, we divided the security
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=155) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
solutions into two main classes: reactive and proactive. Fig. 2 shows
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=165) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the proposed classification analyzed in the following sections. Reactive
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=176) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
or detection-based security solutions use different techniques, includ-
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=186) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ing Statistics, Machine Learning, Deep Learning, and Reinforcement
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=196) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Learning to detect and mitigate attacks. Proactive solutions prevent
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=207) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
attacks by leveraging techniques such as Moving Target Defense, Net-
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=217) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
work Function Virtualization, Cyber Deception, Network Slicing, and
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=228) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Blockchain technology.
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=238) width=82.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Each subclass of defense strategy requires a different level of com-
page=4 block=146428103 (x=282 y=248) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
plexity (computation, storage, and implementation), also in order to
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=259) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
achieve different levels of automation (Fig. 2). The level of automation
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=269) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of the classes can be assessed as a function of their self-* capabili-
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=280) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ties [22]:
page=4 block=146428103 (x=270 y=290) width=32.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
• Self-healing: To automatically detect, diagnose and recover from
page=4 block=119175660 (x=283 y=306) width=238.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
faults that result from internal or external attacks.
page=4 block=119175660 (x=290 y=319) width=179.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
• Self-adaptation: To constantly provide resilience towards internal
page=4 block=119175660 (x=283 y=327) width=238.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and external changes in the networks; the system adapts to detect
page=4 block=119175660 (x=290 y=339) width=231.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
zero-day attacks and AI-enabled adversarial attacks.
page=4 block=119175660 (x=290 y=350) width=185.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
• Self-configuration: To automatically configure the system follow-
page=4 block=119175660 (x=283 y=359) width=238.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ing high-level specifications, and to self-organize into desirable
page=4 block=119175660 (x=290 y=371) width=231.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
structures and/or patterns.
page=4 block=119175660 (x=290 y=381) width=95.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
• Self-optimization: To constantly seek the improvement of the
page=4 block=119175660 (x=283 y=390) width=238.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
system performance and efficiency.
page=4 block=119175660 (x=290 y=402) width=125.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
These parameters are analyzed and compared in the following
page=4 block=146414160 (x=282 y=421) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sections.
page=4 block=146414160 (x=270 y=431) width=30.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
4. SDN-based reactive defense
page=4 block=2052589780 (x=270 y=452) width=116.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
4.1. Threat detection based on statistics
page=4 block=-1923871984 (x=270 y=472) width=134.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The SDN paradigm has high computation capabilities in the con-
page=4 block=146512926 (x=282 y=493) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
troller, which allows the implementation of advanced and effective
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=504) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
methods to detect attacks. Specific attacks, such as DDoS, have been
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=514) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
intensely studied, and their typical behavior can be used to detect them
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=524) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
quickly. For instance, in an exploitation DDoS attack [49], many widely
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=535) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
distributed zombies can attack a host, a segment of the network, or
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=545) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
a service, by flooding the target with packets to exhaust the victims’
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=556) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
bandwidth or resources. Such a DDoS attack can be detected by regis-
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=566) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tering thenumber of packetsdirected to anIP destination and analyzing
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=576) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
this information.
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=587) width=59.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Two methods widely used to characterize flows in networks and
page=4 block=146512926 (x=282 y=597) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
analyze suspicious behaviors are entropy and wavelet transform. The
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=608) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
entropy is a measure of uncertainty of a variable that allows measuring
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=618) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the information gain. The acquisition of information corresponds to the
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=628) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
reduction in entropy. Entropy has been extensively used for anomaly
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=639) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
detection since it enables the detection of changes in traffic distribution
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=649) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of several attacks [50–52]. For instance, during a DDoS attack on a
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=660) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
specific service, there is a significant decrease in the destination IP and
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=670) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
port entropy [53]. During a Port Scan attack, the attacker sends probe
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
packets to a wide range of ports in a specific host to find available ser-
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
vices. In this case, a significant decrease in the source port entropy can
page=4 block=146512926 (x=270 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Page: 5 left:1 upper:1
N.M. Yungaicela-Naula, C. Vargas-Rosales, J.A. Pérez-Díaz et al.
page=5 block=11796484 (x=1 y=1) width=180.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Computer Communications 183 (2022) 64–82
page=5 block=-998244348 (x=393 y=1) width=128.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Fig. 2. Different methods searching for network defense automation with SDN technology.
page=5 block=-2072705688 (x=127 y=346) width=267.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
68
page=5 block=117902879 (x=257 y=723) width=7.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
reveal the attacker if it does not use random source ports [54]. These
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=367) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
anomalies can be identified using predefined thresholds on changes in
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=378) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the entropy values.
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=389) width=68.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The accuracy of the entropy-based approaches can decrease drasti-
page=5 block=16525138 (x=13 y=400) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cally at lower attack rates because the entropy difference in low-volume
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=412) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attacks is almost non-detectable. These threats are frequent in IoT-
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=423) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
based port scanning and DDoS attacks. Given that the thresholds are
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=434) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
defined as fixed parameters during normal traffic conditions, this lack
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=445) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of adaptability can reduce the efficiency of these methods. Finally, a
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=456) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
fundamental parameter to fit is the window size used to calculate the
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=467) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
entropy, which must trade off processing usage and detection accuracy.
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=478) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
It is better to have adaptive window size and threshold.
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=490) width=199.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Wavelet transform is another tool to detect attacks in networks,
page=5 block=16525138 (x=13 y=501) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
particularly in flooding attacks [55]. Wavelet transform is helpful in
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=512) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
data feature extraction or characterization. It transforms data from time
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=523) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to scale domain. This method allows to obtain two kinds of coefficients,
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=534) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
one is the low-frequency coefficient, and the other is thehigh-frequency
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=545) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
coefficient. These coefficients can help to detect attacks, using defined
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=556) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
thresholds to identify the presence of anomalies [56].
page=5 block=16525138 (x=1 y=568) width=191.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Complexity and level of automation
page=5 block=110758735 (x=13 y=579) width=130.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=JIRJGO+CharisSIL-BoldItalic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Statistic-based detection approaches are well explored and have a
page=5 block=16518566 (x=13 y=590) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
relatively low calculation overload and storage demand. These methods
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=601) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
are real-time capable and can handle a large amount of traffic data.
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=612) width=242.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Detection methods based on statistics cannot help to achieve the
page=5 block=16518566 (x=13 y=623) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
self-configuration and self-optimization properties. Although statistical
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=634) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
methods achieve self-healing for known attacks, they do not achieve
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=645) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
self-adaptation. These techniques operate under human supervision and
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=657) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
often fail to detect sophisticated zero-day, polymorphic, and AI-enabled
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=668) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attacks. That fact has driven the security mechanisms to evolve to the
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=679) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
more robust threat detection methods based on models using different
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=690) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
intelligent techniques, presented in the following.
page=5 block=16518566 (x=1 y=701) width=177.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Fig. 3. Attack detection with offline adaptation. For model updating, a new model is
page=5 block=146413820 (x=270 y=492) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
trained offline and it replaces the existing one.
page=5 block=146413820 (x=270 y=501) width=137.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
4.2. Model-based attack detection with offline adaptation
page=5 block=-879883138 (x=270 y=533) width=193.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Model-based methods learn the behavior of a network using histor-
page=5 block=146468918 (x=282 y=554) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ical data, including attacks and non-attack events. These models are
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=565) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
trained once and then used to detect attacks. That is, these methods
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=575) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
learn the system’s behavior offline. Fig. 3 shows the workflow of the
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=586) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
offline learning attack detection approach.
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=596) width=151.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Offline learning is necessary given the high dynamics of networks.
page=5 block=146468918 (x=282 y=607) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
On unexpected and extreme network changes, the models being in use
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=617) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to detect attacks will require updating through offline retraining to
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=628) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
adapt to the new network’s behavior.
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=638) width=133.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Offline learning methods include ML-based and DL-based attack
page=5 block=146468918 (x=282 y=648) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
detection methods.
page=5 block=146468918 (x=270 y=659) width=67.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

to deploy ML algorithms given the higher computational resources in

Signature System (HWDS) [67], catastrophe theory [68], and time-

threats (Fig. 4), they still lack a fundamental factor that could limit

their implementation in production networks, namely the scalabil-

ity. Most of the ML-based solutions have been tested using security
page=6 block=16563992 (x=1 y=690) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
datasets or small test networks. Therefore, the complexity of these
page=6 block=16563992 (x=1 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
techniques is not optimized. This shortage could bring critical problems

when implementing these systems in production environments, such as
page=6 block=146419280 (x=270 y=466) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
unacceptable delays.

4.2.2. DL-based attack detection
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=500) width=110.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
DL algorithms are capable of automatically finding correlations in
page=6 block=146510924 (x=282 y=510) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a high volume of data. For that reason, DL-based techniques have
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=521) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
been used in the last years to detect complex attacks on networks.
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=531) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Several surveys have studied the application of DL for attack detection
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=542) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in SDNs [36–38]. Therefore, we decided not to do redundant work by
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=553) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
reviewingall those works. Instead, we explore the most recent DL-based
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=563) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attack detection proposals.
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=574) width=95.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Fig. 4 shows the application of DL methods to attack detection.

The most common DL methods explored are Deep Belief Propagation
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=595) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(DBP) [70], Stacked Auto Encoder (SAE) [71,72], Convolutional Neural
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=606) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Network (CNN) [73], Multilayer Perceptron (MLP) neural network
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=616) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
[74], Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) neural network [75,76], and
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=627) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU) neural network [77,78]. These methods
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=637) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
demonstrate high accuracy in detecting L3/L4 DDoS attacks. Most in-
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=648) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
terestingly, DL helps to detect complex APTs, namely, application-layer
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=659) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
DDoS attacks, with high accuracy.
page=6 block=146510924 (x=270 y=669) width=122.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
It is noticeable that DL allows higher accuracy than ML in detecting
page=6 block=146510924 (x=282 y=680) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attacks (Fig. 4). However, DL requires more computation and storage

capabilities, which may limit the scalability of the proposed methods.

N.M. Yungaicela-Naula, C. Vargas-Rosales, J.A. Pérez-Díaz et al.

Computer Communications 183 (2022) 64–82

70
