Data analytics, it allows for real-time incident detection and mitigation.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=20) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The controller can dynamically reconfigure the Virtualized Network
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=31) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Security Functions based on the recommendations issued by the Big
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=41) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Data analytic.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=51) width=49.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
MTD using SDN/NFV has also been explored. Work in [111] pro-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=13 y=62) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
poses an SDN/NFV-based MTD mechanism using multiple fuzzy sys-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=72) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tems and a proxy virtual network function (VNF) to achieve DDoS
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=83) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
detection and mitigation. The fuzzy logic is used as an SDN application
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=93) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to detect whether traffic from an external network is DDoS traffic.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=104) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
NFV is used to implement a virtual relay node to prevent DDoS traffic
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=114) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
directly affecting the target server. When an attack is not occurring,
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=125) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
a module called reverse proxy VNF serves as a relay transmission
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=135) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
between the user and the target server, thus transferring the DDoS
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=146) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
attack surface. When an attack occurs, a mechanism based on SDN tech-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=156) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
nology segregates suspicious traffic from legitimate traffic, redirecting
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=167) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
suspicious traffic to a restricted node or dropping them.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=177) width=199.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Work in [112] proposes various NFV-enabled virtual shadow net-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=13 y=188) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
works used in the MTD mechanisms implementation via route mu-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=198) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tation. The aim is to dynamically change the routes for specific re-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=209) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
connaissance packets to harden the attackers’ job of identifying the
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=219) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
current network topology for potential DDoS attacks while enabling
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=230) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the defender to store potential attackers’ information through forensics.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=240) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
This framework offers three MTD strategies to obfuscate the network
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=251) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
topology information for thwarting indirect DDoS attacks at the recon-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=261) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
naissance phase. (1) to mute the routes of the real network to the real
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=272) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
host destination, (2) to use virtual shadow network (VSN) (e.g., virtual
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=282) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
honeypot) as the mirror network and use of one of the VSN as the
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=292) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
destination of the route mutation, and (3) to use the VSN as an overlay
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=303) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
network while the destination of the route mutation is the real host.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=313) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In each cycle, a roulette wheel selection mechanism selects the MTD
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=324) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
strategy using different predefined mutation probabilities. Also, the
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=334) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
VSN placement uses a heuristic topology-aware algorithm.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=345) width=208.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Work in [113] proposes a topology mutation technique combining
page=10 block=16462016 (x=13 y=355) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
NFV and SDN to make the attack surface dynamic. The aim is to create
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=366) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
a virtualized network architecture that can transform itself in real-time
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=376) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
by dynamically introducing virtual components and rearranging the
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=387) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
network’s logical structure. Under this dynamic system, an attacker
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=397) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
finds it complicated and costly to eavesdrop on network packets or
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=408) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
monitor the network traffic to acquire the network topology. Also, the
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=418) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
authors proposed honeypots to obtain a better security response.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=429) width=231.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Additionally, works in [106,114] surveyed other recent SDN/NFV
page=10 block=16462016 (x=13 y=439) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
based security approaches. NFV-based security solutions provide on-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=450) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
demand network programmability, energy efficiency, and mobility sup-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=460) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
port, characteristics that are relevant to IoT networks.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=471) width=193.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
One big concern of the NFV paradigm is that new generation
page=10 block=16462016 (x=13 y=481) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
networks are large-scale and have strict speed, latency, and resource-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=492) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
efficiency requirements. Software-based data plane functions could
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=502) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
introduce unacceptable delays. Therefore, recent studies have proposed
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=513) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
different solutions for high-speed packet processing in user-space, using
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=523) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
common off-the-shield (COTS) hardware [115,116]. In addition, it is
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=533) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
important to mention the vector packet processing (VPP) [115,117]
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=544) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
framework that is a kernel-bypass method, which provides flexibility
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=554) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(multiprogramming) with hardware-comparable packet processing per-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=565) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
formance by using techniques of batch processing. VPP can leverage
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=575) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a general-purpose CPU to process packets in a vectorized fashion,
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=586) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
which is highly efficient. Consequently, VPP provides full network
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=596) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
functionalities of layers 2, 3, and above. The nature of VPP-enabled
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=607) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
VNFs will provide the open-access, software-based, scalable, and high-
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=617) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
performance capabilities to deploy automated security solutions in
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=628) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
SDN-based networks.
page=10 block=16462016 (x=1 y=638) width=75.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Complexity and level of automation
page=10 block=110759085 (x=13 y=649) width=130.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=JIRJGO+CharisSIL-BoldItalic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
NFV promises several advantages to defend the networks proac-
page=10 block=16481168 (x=13 y=659) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tively. However, it can demand high computation and storage re-
page=10 block=16481168 (x=1 y=670) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sources. In addition, NFV has been presented before, jointly with
page=10 block=16481168 (x=1 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
other mechanisms, to provide self-configuration and self-optimization
page=10 block=16481168 (x=1 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
capabilities. That is, in combination with other strategies studied in
page=10 block=16481168 (x=1 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
this paper, NFV can potentially provide complete security automation.
page=10 block=146408384 (x=270 y=20) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
However, research in this area is still emerging. Therefore, many chal-
page=10 block=146408384 (x=270 y=31) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
lenges remain unsolved, such as optimal selection and deployment of
page=10 block=146408384 (x=270 y=41) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SDN/NFV-based security mechanisms and optimal orchestration over
page=10 block=146408384 (x=270 y=51) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
different network domains [3].
page=10 block=146408384 (x=270 y=62) width=110.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
5.3. Cyber deception
page=10 block=1238630898 (x=270 y=83) width=70.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Another type of proactive defense mechanism is cyber deception.
page=10 block=146470136 (x=282 y=104) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Deception tools distribute a collection of traps and decoys across a
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=115) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
system architecture to imitate genuine assets. Unlike MTD, deception
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=125) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
methods introduce uncertainty for the attacker without changing the
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=135) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
actual surface but instead creating a virtual attack surface [118]. They
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=146) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
provide adversaries with an incorrect view of the target system so
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=156) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
that attacks based on such wrong information are likely to fail and
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=167) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
present an opportunity for detection [118,119]. Examples of deception
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=177) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tools are simple honeypot systems. Modern deception tools require
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=188) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
analytics and automation methods to make them simple to deploy
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=198) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
but effective for thwarting attacks. The capabilities of SDN provide
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=209) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
an opportunity to design an optimized and autonomous solution for
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=219) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
network defense, which can permit advanced use cases of the decep-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=230) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tion platform such as integrated, proactive threat hunting, and active
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=240) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
attacker engagement [120].
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=251) width=99.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Works in [121,122] proposed reconnaissance deception systems
page=10 block=146470136 (x=282 y=261) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
(RCSs) to defend from malicious discovery and reconnaissance attacks
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=272) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in computer networks. These systems use SDN to simulate complete
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=282) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
virtual network topology, thus achieving an effective defense strategy
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=293) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
against adversarial reconnaissance. Work in [123] proposes an SDN-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=303) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
based framework for unobtrusively integrating decoy services into
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=314) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
production workloads. The SDN technology divides a production net-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=324) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
work into segments of production and decoy. Attackers are redirected
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=335) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to decoys, which have the same configuration as the protected pro-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=345) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
duction system. Therefore, the attackers never reach the protected
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=355) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
assets.
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=366) width=22.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
MimePot [124] is a cyber–physical honeypot conceived for indus-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=282 y=376) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
trial control networks. This security framework can simulate physical
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=387) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
processes operations of Industrial Control Systems to capture intricate
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=397) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cyber–physical attacks using SDN technology. Work in [118] proposes
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=408) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a cyber deception system to combat cyber adversaries in a wireless vir-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=418) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tualization framework. In this wireless framework, the SDN controller
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=429) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
continuously monitors the network traffic, observes the connections,
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=439) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and creates mobile virtual network operators (MVNO). Additionally,
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=450) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
it directs cyber adversaries towards deception MVNO. Work in [125]
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=460) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
proposes a security framework with adaptive defense. This solution
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=471) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
protects key nodes based on game theory (GT) through the formulation
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=481) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of a multistage game by using the architectural advantages of SDN.
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=492) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Moreover, it considers deceptive behaviors of the cyber defender in
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=502) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
different stages.
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=513) width=56.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Work in [126] presents a cyber deception solution that implements
page=10 block=146470136 (x=282 y=523) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
defenses against penetration attacks using a decoy chain development
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=534) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(DCD) method based on the combination of NFV and SDN. In this
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=544) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
defense architecture, the SDN controller monitors the security status
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=554) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of the whole network and can detect possible attack sources using
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=565) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Intrusion Detection System (IDS) technology. Moreover, using SDN
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=575) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
technology, the DCD can deploy decoy chains in the generic servers
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=586) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on the DP to change the attack surface, based on the global network
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=596) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
status and under resource constraints and multiple attack sources. NFV
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=607) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
helps to deploy dynamic service delivery in the network.
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=617) width=203.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The goal of the security framework KAGE [127] goes beyond the
page=10 block=146470136 (x=282 y=628) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
implementation of sophisticated honeypots or MTD mechanisms to dy-

namically defend the system. KAGE expanded into a new area of active

defensive deception, where the adversary’s decision loop is deliberately

manipulated by proactive and reactive adaptation of the environment
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=670) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as KAGE learns about the capabilities and intents of the client that
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
KAGE is engaging. That is, a detected intrusion does not result in block-
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing or shutting down connections. Instead, KAGE seamlessly moves the
page=10 block=146470136 (x=270 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

the resources. However, this area still lacks further development. The
page=11 block=16457100 (x=1 y=84) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
costs and benefits of deception in defense need rigorous evaluations
page=11 block=16457100 (x=1 y=94) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
before practical use [127,128]. For instance, a cost would result in

page=11 block=16457100 (x=1 y=115) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
virtual subnets and the increased delay of sending packets through

virtual devices. The proper allocation of honeypots in the network to

to be further explored.

capabilities. However, this area requires further effort.

5.4. Network slicing (NS)
page=11 block=5702838 (x=1 y=201) width=87.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Network slicing strategies promise effective solutions such as dy-
page=11 block=16479195 (x=13 y=223) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
namic QoS/QoE management [27] for IoT and 5G/6G [7,129] tech-
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=234) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
nologies. Network slicing allows to isolate different traffics to maintain
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=244) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
security and reliability in the network [3]. Each slice is isolated and
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=255) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
has diverse network functions and virtual resources. Thus, individual
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=265) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
network applications and services can run on each slice, with different
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=276) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
levels of security required by individual slices. The combination of
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=287) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
SDN and NFV on network slicing can support the design of advanced
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=297) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
security solutions with fined-grained security features, such as per-
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=308) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
application traffic flow [129,130]. Still, further research efforts are
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=318) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
required to achieve the required granularity protection, with dynamic
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=329) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and flexible management, and at the same time maintaining a low cost
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=339) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of implementation.
page=11 block=16479195 (x=1 y=350) width=67.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Complexity and level of automation
page=11 block=110757645 (x=13 y=361) width=130.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=JIRJGO+CharisSIL-BoldItalic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Network slicing, combined with SDN and NFV, promises to enable
page=11 block=16475135 (x=13 y=371) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
all self-* capabilities needed to obtain a fully automated solution.
page=11 block=16475135 (x=1 y=382) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Furthermore, all techniques previously studied, including reactive and
page=11 block=16475135 (x=1 y=392) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
proactive solutions, can be integrated into each slice, thus providing
page=11 block=16475135 (x=1 y=403) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a comprehensive security solution for new generation networks. How-
page=11 block=16475135 (x=1 y=414) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ever, this area is emerging, and strong efforts are required to obtain
page=11 block=16475135 (x=1 y=424) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
such solutions.
page=11 block=16475135 (x=1 y=435) width=52.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
5.5. Blockchain-assisted SDN
page=11 block=6490806 (x=1 y=457) width=99.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In recent years, Blockchain technology has been explored to design
page=11 block=16558748 (x=13 y=479) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
more scalable, efficient, decentralized, secure, and trustworthy SDN
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=489) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
architectures [131–135]. Blockchain enables verification information
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=500) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
transactions via distributed network authorization and, subsequently,
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=511) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
it adds that data to an unalterable ledger. That is, unknown entities
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=521) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
can communicate with each other without a trusted third party (con-
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=532) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
troller). The decentralization eliminates control by a single controller,
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=542) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
removing the risk of single-point failures. In addition, since each infor-
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=553) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
mation transaction is public, this technology offers a reliable and easily
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=564) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
verifiable infrastructure [136–138].
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=574) width=126.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Given the decentralization property and continuous nodes/users/
page=11 block=16558748 (x=13 y=585) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
information-transaction verification, blockchain can help to preventthe
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=595) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
most common and disruptive attacks in SDN-based networks, namely,
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=606) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
DDoS attacks. The primary use of this technology is to solve the
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=616) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
consensus and synchronization problem of multiple distributed SDN
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=627) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
controllers [132,139] and enable trustworthy controller/switch associ-
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=638) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ations [140,141]. Nevertheless, we consider blockchain as a proactive
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=648) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mechanism since it ensures the executed transactions in the network
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=659) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
must be approved and verified by all participating nodes, which cre-
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=669) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ates irrefutable records to avoid data tampering and double-spending
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
problems and ensure ledger consistency [139,142]. For instance, the
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
blockchain technology integration into SDN provides capabilities to
page=11 block=16558748 (x=1 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
prevent IoT devices from being used as botnets that launch DDoS
page=11 block=146407744 (x=270 y=20) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
attacks [143].
page=11 block=146407744 (x=270 y=31) width=49.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Complexity and level of automation
page=11 block=-1892417290 (x=282 y=41) width=130.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=JIRJGO+CharisSIL-BoldItalic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Blockchain-enabled SDN is an emergent area of study. Still, many
page=11 block=146417879 (x=282 y=51) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
challenges remain unsolved in thistechnology [136]. A relevant issue is
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=62) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=72) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=83) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
uncertainty for participating nodes. Moreover, storing a blockchain in
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=93) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
OF switches requires memory resources in large numbers, which can
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=104) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
become a bottleneck as the size of the blockchain increases [144].
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=114) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
In addition, the cost of computational power and energy of some

consensus mechanisms can be prohibitive for organizations or groups
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=135) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
interested in adopting blockchain technology.
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=146) width=163.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Finally, blockchain is adaptive to new internal or external changes
page=11 block=146417879 (x=282 y=156) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
making it more robust to novel and complex attacks. Also, this strategy
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=167) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
can enable security self-configuration. Self-optimized security solutions
page=11 block=146417879 (x=270 y=177) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
using blockchain and SDN require further development.

6.1. Complexity and level of automation

As described before, the complexity analysis includes the computa-

work threats. Nevertheless, each technique can solve different levels

of sophistication of these attacks. Statistical methods can solve simple

performance for network layer (high-volume) DDoS threats. DL ap-

proaches demonstrate better results for application-layer attacks such

as low-rate, slow-rate, and one-hit DDoS attacks. Online adaptation, RL,

and adversarial learning techniques can defeat even more sophisticated

APTs, including AI-enabled threats.

page=13 block=8847990 (x=5 y=126) width=27.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
Proactive solutions occur more proper to protect new generation
page=13 block=16485702 (x=13 y=169) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
networks, such as IoT, 5G, 6G, vehicular ad-hoc networks (VANETS),
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) networks [148], etc. These networks
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=190) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
have in common that they are heterogeneous, dynamic, and large-scale,
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
making them hard to meet the strict requirements, such as low latency,
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=211) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
high reliability, high mobility, top security, and enormous connectivity.
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
DDoS attacks are the most common and disruptive threats in these
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=231) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
transitional networks. Other threats treated by proactive solutions are
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=242) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
eavesdrop, sniffer, botnets prevention. The heterogeneity of the devices
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=252) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
could hinder the use of reactive solutions in these networks since
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=263) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
they can expose to numerous FPs. Thus, preventing threats rather than
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=273) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
mitigating them could be a better strategy in these complex systems.
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=284) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
We also observed that almost all proactive security solutions work in
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=294) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
combinations. Examples of these combinations are MTD-NFV, MTD-
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=304) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Cyber Deception, NF-Network Slicing, MTD-NFV-Cyber Deception, and
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=315) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Blockchain assisted-NFV-Network Slicing-NFV, as shown in Table 3.
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=325) width=242.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Finally, we highlight that proactive and reactive solutions can be
page=13 block=16485702 (x=13 y=336) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
combined for their application in different scenarios. For instance, ML
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=346) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and DL can enable more effective and optimized proactive solutions.
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=357) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
And conversely, NFV could bring the automation capability to the
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=367) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
reactive solutions.
page=13 block=16485702 (x=1 y=378) width=64.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
7. Discussion
page=13 block=3344724 (x=1 y=398) width=51.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
We studied reactive and proactive strategies that pursue security
page=13 block=16548420 (x=13 y=419) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
automation in SDN-based networks. Applying these solutions to di-
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=430) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
minish the effects of attacks is powerful, but those solutions can have
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=440) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
some drawbacks that sophisticated attacks can exploit to beat them.
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=451) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Once the attacker gains access to the system, proactive solutions can-
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=461) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
not thwart them. Therefore, the detection-based mechanisms remain
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=471) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
relevant. Worthwhile approaches can combine reactive and proactive
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=482) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
strategies to automate the security solution in SDN-based networks. An
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=492) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
automatic security solution can follow the rule: Prevent everything you
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=503) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
can, everything you cannot prevent, investigate, detect, and mitigate fast.
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=513) width=244.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The SDN-based security solution for networks is expected to beauto-
page=13 block=16548420 (x=13 y=524) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
mated in the coming years. Such solutions must consider all advantages
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=534) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and limitations of the SDN architecture. A solution proposal that does
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=545) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
not consider the effects on the network performance, such as the bot-
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=555) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tleneck to collect packets for analysis, will not be scalable. Also, there
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=565) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
is a need for a proper evaluation of the network defense mechanisms.
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=576) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The proposals reviewed in this survey used partial and different metrics
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=586) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to assess their efficiency in protecting networks. A good practice is to
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=597) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
use a standard evaluation system of security solutions for networks
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=607) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
based on the SDN paradigm. Next, we analyze the scalability and
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=618) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
assessment factors, which we consider essential elements to different
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=628) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
security proposals before their implementation in production networks.
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=639) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Finally, we discuss open problems to manage automation.
page=13 block=16548420 (x=1 y=649) width=206.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
7.1. Scalable SDN-based security solutions
page=13 block=9374998 (x=1 y=670) width=143.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Most of the SDN-based security solutions revised throughout this
page=13 block=16460592 (x=13 y=691) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
survey propose to perform main processes in the AP. The controller
page=13 block=16460592 (x=1 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Areas of application
page=13 block=1585053891 (x=417 y=39) width=58.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Next-generation Firewalls in
page=13 block=-1808071296 (x=417 y=64) width=81.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Datacenters, Campus Networks,

page=13 block=-1808071296 (x=417 y=81) width=84.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
IoT, UAV networks, 5G/6G,
page=13 block=-2108684750 (x=417 y=122) width=80.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(VANETs)
page=13 block=-2108684750 (x=417 y=130) width=28.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
is in charge of capturing data from DP through an SBI protocol and
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=169) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
deploying the mitigation strategies on the physical devices. In this
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
way, the intelligent approach leverages the computational and storage
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=190) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
resources of the SDN controller, as well as its global visibility and
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
centralized control.
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=210) width=68.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
However, this approach can limit the scalability of the different
page=13 block=146432943 (x=282 y=221) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
security solutions and even introduce new vulnerabilities to DoS/DDoS
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=231) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attacks. That is, as the traffic demand of modern networks increases,
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=242) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
analyzing all of these packets with a security system at wire speeds
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=252) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
will be challenging using sophisticated algorithms, ensuring at the same
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=262) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
time high accuracy.
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=273) width=70.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Effective SDN-based security solutions require considering the op-
page=13 block=146432943 (x=282 y=283) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
timization of different resources to provide scalability. Fig. 6 shows
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=294) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
specific strategies to add scalability to SDN-based security solutions,
page=13 block=146432943 (x=270 y=304) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
which we discuss in the following.

7.1.1. Traffic sampling
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=335) width=78.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Sampling techniques reduce the flow of data gathering from the
page=13 block=146454659 (x=282 y=345) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
controller and minimize the communication required between switches
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=356) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and the controller, thus easing the CP overload in the vast network
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=366) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
traffic condition. The sampling period is critical in sampling strate-
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=377) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
gies. The samples’ requests must be periodic in short time intervals
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=387) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
since long intervals are impractical for accurate and rapid detection
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=397) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
of anomalies. The attack events appearing over a time scale inferior to
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=408) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the one selected for traffic sampling will make the security control rule
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=418) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
useless.
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=429) width=26.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
Improvements to the sampling strategy include adaptive flow collec-
page=13 block=146454659 (x=282 y=439) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tion [149]. The improper implementation of sampling methods com-
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=449) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
promises the accuracy of threats detection, given the relevant flows
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=460) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
missing probability.
page=13 block=146454659 (x=270 y=470) width=70.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
7.1.2. Stateful SDN
page=13 block=146445722 (x=270 y=491) width=67.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
We can solve the scalability of the Open SDN architecture by keep-
page=13 block=146445722 (x=282 y=501) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ing data flows in the DP as much as possible to maintain the controller’s
page=13 block=146445722 (x=270 y=512) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
safety and reduce the burden of the controller and SBI bandwidth.
page=13 block=146445722 (x=270 y=522) width=237.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In a stateful SDN architecture, the switches make independent for-
page=13 block=146445722 (x=282 y=532) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
warding decisions based on their local flow state information without
page=13 block=146445722 (x=270 y=543) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
contacting the controller [150]. The facts that supported the develop-
page=13 block=146445722 (x=270 y=553) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ment of the stateful SDN architecture include:
page=13 block=146445722 (x=270 y=564) width=164.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(i) The computational resources available on current switches are
page=13 block=136617044 (x=281 y=580) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sufficient to extend their functionalities, adding a certain level
page=13 block=136617044 (x=294 y=590) width=227.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of intelligence;
page=13 block=136617044 (x=294 y=601) width=52.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(ii) Some threats, such as scanning, sniffing, and DoS attacks, can be
page=13 block=136617044 (x=279 y=612) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
locally identified and neutralized; and
page=13 block=136617044 (x=294 y=622) width=136.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(iii) The centralized control of stateful SDN architecture remains as
page=13 block=136617044 (x=276 y=633) width=245.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in the Open SDN architecture.
page=13 block=136617044 (x=294 y=643) width=106.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Stateful architecture can helpto effectively reduce the impact onthe
page=13 block=146438444 (x=282 y=660) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
network performance during DoS/DDoS attacks [151–156]. Further,
page=13 block=146438444 (x=270 y=670) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the P4 technology [157] and VPP-based VNFs will enable intelligence
page=13 block=146438444 (x=270 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in switches, to obtain an open-access, automated and scalable security
page=13 block=146438444 (x=270 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
solution.

7.1.3. Cooperative control and data plane
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=129) width=142.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
It is important to note that sophisticated attacks, such as slow DDoS
page=14 block=16475723 (x=13 y=139) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
attacks, might be troublesome to detect using local switches’ informa-
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=150) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tion, meaning that stateful SDN architecture could not be efficient. A
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=160) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cooperative intelligence between DP and CP could potentially minimize
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=170) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the effects of advanced DoS/DDoS attacks on network performance [89,
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=181) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
158,159]. In this approach, the stateful switches commonly perform
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=191) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
coarse-grained detection to determine suspicious flows and send them
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=202) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to the controller. The controller leverages its computation capabilities
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=212) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to perform fine-grained classification of attacks and propose defense
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=222) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
strategies. Finally, the switches react to mitigate the attacks, according
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=233) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to rules installed by the controller.
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=243) width=124.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
It is essential to design the cooperation mechanisms between the DP
page=14 block=16475723 (x=13 y=254) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and CP carefully and effectively. Switches with some additional/special
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=264) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
modules or a new component like DP cache, will increase the network
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=275) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
complexity and the overall cost [34]. Moreover, it will be unrealistic to
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=285) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
run complex detection algorithms, such as ML, DL, MTD, etc., within
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the switches. Usually, the SDN controller runs these algorithms. Nev-
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=306) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ertheless, switches could only perform coarse-grained attack detection
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and take independent actions if required.
page=14 block=16475723 (x=1 y=327) width=147.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
7.1.4. Distributed SDN deployment
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=347) width=118.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Distributing monitoring capability in a multi-controller environ-
page=14 block=16509567 (x=13 y=358) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ment [160–162] could also enable the scalability of an SDN-based
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
security solution. The architecture of multi-controllers can operate on
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=379) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
either a two-level hierarchical controller or a distributed controller
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
system.
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=399) width=26.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
Hierarchical or distributed architectures face the bottleneck prob-
page=14 block=16509567 (x=13 y=410) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lem when the network size becomes large, and the applications running
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=420) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
require advanced processing and analysis to operate their function-
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=431) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
alities in the network. Hierarchical controller architecture can still
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=441) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
produce bottlenecks for large networks, and the distributed controller
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=451) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
scheme can present synchronization overheads [153]. Besides, in net-
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=462) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
work environments that require diversity in SDN controllers, such as
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=472) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in IoT, having a backup controller for each controller may become
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=483) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
costly, thus resulting in non-scalable and complex solutions [163].
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=493) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The optimized deployment of multi-controller environments is still an
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=504) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
unsolved challenge.
page=14 block=16509567 (x=1 y=514) width=70.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
7.1.5. NFV
page=14 block=2493043 (x=1 y=535) width=38.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The emerging NFV technology can enable the scalability and flex-
page=14 block=16492591 (x=13 y=545) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ibility factors of SDN-based security solutions since NFV deploys dif-
page=14 block=16492591 (x=1 y=555) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ferent virtual functions (VFs) on-demand and on the fly. In addition,
page=14 block=16492591 (x=1 y=566) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
NFV enables openness through vendor lock-in alleviation, which is

currently a factor affecting the deployment of holistic, comprehensive,
page=14 block=16492591 (x=1 y=587) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and flexible automated solutions.
page=14 block=16492591 (x=1 y=597) width=118.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
All the strategies discussed can be combined to obtain a scalable
page=14 block=16492591 (x=13 y=608) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and automated security solution.

7.2. Evaluation of SDN-based solutions
page=14 block=8653947 (x=1 y=639) width=132.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
It is tough to find many studies with an evaluation that is both
page=14 block=16481168 (x=13 y=659) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
comprehensive and realistic. Various works give many results on a wide
page=14 block=16481168 (x=1 y=670) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
range of performance metrics, using small test networks, lacking the
page=14 block=16481168 (x=1 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
realism of implementation. Other works try to build a large topology
page=14 block=16481168 (x=1 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
using simulation tools but run into some issues due to the limitations of
page=14 block=16481168 (x=1 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the environment. Without a strong evaluation, one cannot fully assess
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=129) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the benefit and overhead associated with a new system, so the practical
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=140) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
applicability of a given solution usually remains in question [39].
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=150) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
The evaluation of critical parameters such as the traffic added by the
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=161) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
method, the processing and memory demands, the changes require-
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=172) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ments in the current architecture of SDN, or the level of automation
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=183) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of the proposed method, can help to define if the technique provides
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=194) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
crucial benefits when deployed in a production network.
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=204) width=202.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The most common metrics used to validate SDN-based security
page=14 block=146481986 (x=282 y=215) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
solutions for networks, particularly those based on the threat detec-
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=226) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tion/mitigation approach, are accuracy, recall, F-score, FPs rate, ROC
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=237) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
curve, and detection rate. Since these metrics are related to the clas-
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=248) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sifier performance used on the threat detection, they only allow a
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=258) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
partial assessment of the overall conditions existing in the network.
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=269) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Besides using the classification metrics, we can evaluate the SDN-based
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=280) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
security solutions using other performance metrics, as described in the

following.

Table 6 defines a set of parameters and metrics to evaluate the
page=14 block=146481986 (x=282 y=312) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
network security design using SDN effectively.
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=323) width=165.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Firstly, reactive solutions are mainly evaluated based on the perfor-
page=14 block=146481986 (x=282 y=334) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
mance of threats detection and mitigation. In addition to the standard
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=345) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
classification metrics, we can quantify the preprocessing effectiveness,
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=356) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the models’ training time, the robustness on attack detection, the ratio
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=366) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of malicious traffic blocked, and the legitimate packet loss rate.
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=377) width=227.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The flexibility parameter is also significant since integral security

solutions will provide a more complete and holistic system to defend
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=399) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the network.
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=410) width=45.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Moreover, all security proposal designs must consider the scalability
page=14 block=146481986 (x=282 y=420) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
component. A sophisticated security system could not be scalable,
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=431) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
whereas a simple one could be ineffective for advanced attacks. The
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mechanism of security must have a trade off between effectiveness and
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=453) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
scalability. Direct metrics to assess the scalability property of a security
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=464) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
solution include the end-to-end communication delay, the controller
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=474) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
CPU and memory usage, the switches TCAM usage, the throughput, and
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=485) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the additional traffic on the network. The analysis of the algorithms’
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=496) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
complexity (preprocessing, detection, and mitigation algorithms) using
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=507) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
big O notation can be helpful in the proper definition of the scalability
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=518) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
factor of a specific solution.
page=14 block=146481986 (x=270 y=528) width=99.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The effectiveness and scalability of a security solution must not

with automatic tools.

can evaluate the ability of a solution to enable specific self-* properties.

effectively and efficiently carry out incident responses.

A fundamental evaluation parameter is the implementation and
