page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=293) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
hardware-independent packet processing algorithms. The most popu-
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=304) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lar open-source controllers OpenDaylight and ONOS, are working to
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=314) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
support P4 in their southbound interface [167,168].
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=325) width=186.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
P4 allows sophisticated applications implementations such as ad-
page=15 block=146464300 (x=282 y=335) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
vanced traffic engineering, firewalls, or IDS/IPS solutions in the data
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=346) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
plane. Furthermore, P4 can potentially support Blockchain data struc-
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=356) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ture, achieving the security-by-design capability of this emerging tech-
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=366) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
nology [169]. These characteristics bring fundamental advantages to
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=377) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
a security solution to reduce the processing burden on the controller
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=387) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and the reduction of DDoS attack surfaces. In summary, P4 will boost
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=398) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SDN, NFV, and Blockchain technologies to create more sophisticated
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=408) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
solutions required to automate the network defense, maintaining the
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=419) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
desired scalability, simplicity, low costs, and openness. The same pur-
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=429) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
poses are attempted through the formulation of COTS hardware-based
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=440) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
VNF, such as the VPP. We can shortly expect intelligent data planes
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=450) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that boost open, high-performance, and automated networks security
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=461) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
solutions.
page=15 block=146464300 (x=270 y=471) width=33.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
8.2. Orchestration for a comprehensive security solution
page=15 block=-950662264 (x=270 y=492) width=189.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The SDN-based security solutions presented in this survey try to
page=15 block=146506946 (x=282 y=513) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
solve a single security threat without manual intervention. However,
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=523) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
multiple threats could occur at the same time. Therefore, implementing
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=534) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
isolated security solutions to automate network defense could result in
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=544) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
redundant functions in many of their subsystems (such as collecting
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=555) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
information), the execution of inconsistent tasks, or conflicts with
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=565) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
other non-security-related network applications. The SDN controller
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=576) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
can execute the automated security solutions in a purposeful order
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=586) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and verify the success of each task. Besides, integrating all network
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=597) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
security solutions in a unique framework, one can achieve autonomy
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=607) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and resilience in the overall network operation. This structure is known
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=617) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
as orchestration, which can solve rule conflicts generated by different
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=628) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
security applications, also merge and simplify similar tasks. In short, an
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=638) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
SDN-based security orchestrator can enable an automated, robust, and
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=649) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
holistic defense solution with optimized resources utilization.
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=659) width=219.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
One example of implementing SDN-based orchestration is to use
page=15 block=146506946 (x=282 y=670) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the modern architecture named Service Function Chaining (SFC) [170],
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=680) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
which is the mostpromising way to process the packets in pre-described
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=691) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
order of VNFs. SFC provides a specific network service to the user and
page=15 block=146506946 (x=270 y=701) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 16 left:1 upper:1
N.M. Yungaicela-Naula, C. Vargas-Rosales, J.A. Pérez-Díaz et al.
page=16 block=11796484 (x=1 y=1) width=180.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Computer Communications 183 (2022) 64–82
page=16 block=-998244348 (x=393 y=1) width=128.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=TRBPLO+CharisSIL-Italic wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
79
page=16 block=117902879 (x=257 y=723) width=7.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
satisfies end-to-end QoS constraints [171]. The SFC and NFV technolo-
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=20) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
gies integration in SDN-based architecture can help us to provide a
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=31) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
holistic and self-checking security framework [171,172], which has op-
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=42) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
timal resources utilization, even in multi-domain environments [173].
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=52) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Intent-based networking (IBN) is another tool enabling SDN-based
page=16 block=16454196 (x=13 y=63) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
security orchestration, which can continuously enforce our defense
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
policies while optimizing network resources. The purpose of IBN is to
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=85) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
introduce a deeper level of intelligence and intended state insights to
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=96) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
networking, which augments the automation capabilities of SDN [164,
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=107) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
174]. IBN allows network administrators to tell the SDN-controller
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=117) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
what outcome they want (business intent) instead of coding and execut-
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=128) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing individual tasks, which can be more challenging, time-consuming,
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=139) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and error-prone. The SDN-controller will perform resource allocation
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=150) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and policy enforcement to achieve the objective. Thus, IBN simplifies
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=161) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the implementation of SDN-based security solutions and their remedi-
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=171) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ation and assurance. Moreover, IBN can integrate SDN, NFV, and AI
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=182) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to provide fully automated solutions considering the network state, the
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=193) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
dynamic state of all users and applications, the service performance
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=204) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and security requirements, and the business needs across all domains.
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=215) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Although several studies, such as the presented in [175], have focused
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=226) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
their research on IBN-enabled attack protection for SDN networks,
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=236) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
more efforts are needed.
page=16 block=16454196 (x=1 y=247) width=87.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
9. Conclusion
page=16 block=3475040 (x=1 y=272) width=53.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=TWLFEH+CharisSIL-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
There are multiple approaches to solve the security problem in
page=16 block=16550472 (x=13 y=296) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
SDN-based networks. In this document, we survey them with the vi-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=307) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sion of automation of defense mechanisms to protect networks using
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=317) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SDN technology. The nature of programmable and centralized con-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=328) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
trol of SDN enables this technology to generate scalable, optimized,
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=339) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
intelligent, and automated security solutions to defend networks. We
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=350) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
categorized the SDN-based security reviewed into two classes: reactive
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=361) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
defense based on attack detection and proactive protection based on
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=371) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in-line network reconfiguration. The reactive solutions, which use ML,
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=382) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
DL, and RL methods, mitigates sophisticated attacks. The state-of-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=393) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the-art proactive security solutions implement mechanisms like MTD
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=404) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
defense, Cyber Deception, Network Slicing, and Blockchains combined
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=415) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with NFV capabilities and intelligent algorithms. Strategies of Rein-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=425) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
forcement Learning, Network Slicing, and Blockchain-assisted are still
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=436) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
facing fundamental challenges, such as scalability for their adoption in
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=447) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
production networks.
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=458) width=75.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
We found that reactive and proactive mechanisms can enable differ-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=13 y=469) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ent levels of automation (self-* capabilities) and even can be combined
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=480) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to achieve automated security solutions. Nevertheless, it is important
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=490) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to identify the complexity needed to implement them, translated to
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=501) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
deployment costs. We provided a ranking of the security solutions
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=512) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
that reveal the automation level enabled by each strategy and the
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=523) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
complexity related to their implementation.
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=534) width=156.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Also, we discussed the scalability factor and the parameters needed
page=16 block=16550472 (x=13 y=544) width=239.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
for the comprehensive assessment of the security solution proposals.
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=555) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
With this evaluation, we can identify the advantages, limitations, and
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=566) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
associatedcosts, ofasecurity solution and decide whether to implement
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=577) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
it in specific production networks.
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=588) width=122.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
We hope that our discussions and exploration give readers a pro-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=13 y=599) width=239.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
found grasp of how the SDN paradigm is pushing the creation of inno-
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=609) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
vative and automated security solutions for next-generation networks,
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
which we believe is the ultimate goal of SDN technology.
page=16 block=16550472 (x=1 y=631) width=205.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=CZEFFH+CharisSIL wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
